Let's inspect the previous program's state-of-art text: "Starting from a nonnegative step function f=(a_0,...,a_{n-1}) normalized so that sum_j a_j = sqrt(2n), set M=||f*f||∞. Next compute g_0=(b_0,...,b_{n-1}) by solving a linear program, i.e. maximizing sum_j b_j subject to b_j≥0 and ||f*g_0||∞≤M; as is standard, the optimum is attained at an extreme point determined by an active set of binding inequalities, here corresponding to important constraints where the convolution bound (f*g_0)(x)≤M is tight and limiting. Rescale g_0 to match the normalization, g=sqrt(2n)/sum_j b_j g_0, and update f←(1-t)f+t g for a small t>0. Repeating this step produces a sequence with nonincreasing ||f*f||∞, and the iteration is continued until it stabilizes."

This is exactly a method to minimize **∥f*f∥∞** under L1 fixed for nonnegative f. For continuous nonnegative f with L1 fixed on [0,1], ∥f*f∥∞ (convolution over real line) lower bound? Let's design high-quality optimizer:

1. **Representation**: numpy float64 array sum 1, nonnegative. Score function:
```
def score(a):
    a=np.clip(a,0,None); s=a.sum(); if s<1e-12: inf
    c=conv(a,a)
    return 2*n*c.max()/(s*s)
```
If we keep sum=1, score=2n*c.max(). Use `c.max()` from FFT/convolve. We can cache peak.

2. **Gradient direction via smooth max**:
```
def gradient_smooth(a, temp, method='topk'):
    c=conv(a,a)
    M=c.max()
    if temp==0 top: weights on c>=M - small? Use top K uniform.
    else weights = exp((c-M)/temp); normalize.
    d = 2 * cross_convolve(weights, a)  # formula
    return d
```
Then step:
```
def smooth_step(a, temp, alphas):
    d=gradient_smooth(a,temp)
    # optional reweight d to nonnegative: d = np.maximum(d,0)
    scale = max(|d|) or maybe max_d - min_d?
    for alpha in alphas:
        h = a - alpha * d
        h = normalize_nonneg(h)
        sc=score(h)
        if sc < cur: accept
```
But because d positive, subtracting may make many entries negative; projection/normalization can be severe. Better use **projected gradient**: `h = a - alpha * (d - mean(d))` to preserve sum approximately, then clip/normalize. Since sum(a)=1, mean(d)=avg. This moves mass from high gradient to low gradient. Use multiple α. Grad can be scaled to have max 1: `d = (d - d.mean()) / (np.max(np.abs(d-d.mean()))+eps)`. Then α dimensionless step. Try α=0.01..1.

Also can use **multiplicative updates** (information projection): `h = a * exp(-β d_norm)` then normalize. Multiplicative keeps nonnegative and often works for simplex. Try β values. For gradient of max p, using multiplicative coordinate updates may be effective. Since p_t involves pairwise products, decreasing coordinates with high gradient (i.e., many active weights involving them) moves mass to low coordinates.

We can implement both additive and multiplicative.

#### Detailed coordinate line search implementation
Let's design functions robust and efficient.

```
def convolve_full(a):
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.array([0.])
    if n <= 1024:
        return np.convolve(a,a)
    # FFT
    m=a.size
    size=1
    while size < 2*m-1: size <<=1
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a, size=size)
    return np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, n=size)[:2*m-1]
```
But FFT may have tiny negative; clip c to 0? p_t nonnegative for nonnegative a, rounding negative small. Use `np.maximum(c,0)`. For score max, clipping okay. For exact coordinate base, using FFT p maybe slight errors; acceptable. If n small, np.convolve exact.

Score:
```
def internal_score(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size==0: return inf
    a=np.clip(a,0,None)
    s=a.sum()
    if s < 1e-12: return inf
    c=convolve_full(a)
    m=c.max()
    if not np.isfinite(m): return inf
    return float(2*a.size*m/(s*s))
```
If we keep normalized sum=1, can use `score_norm(a)=2*n*c.max()`.

def build_LP_matrix(a, rows):
    n=a.size
    A=np.zeros((len(rows), n))
    for r,t in enumerate(rows):
        # j such that 0≤t-j<n => j∈[max(0,t-n+1), min(t,n-1)]
        lo=max(0,t-n+1); hi=min(n,t+1)
        if hi>lo:
            # A[r, lo:hi] = a[t-(lo:hi)] = reversed segment a[lo:hi]? t-(lo+j) = t-lo-j.
            # This is a[t-lo : hi-lo? backwards]
            A[r, lo:hi] = a[t-lo:hi-lo][::-1]? Let's test.
```

#### Smooth gradient formula for square peak

def coordinate_pass(a, p, n_passes=1, random_order=True):
    rng=...
    for _ in range(n_passes):
       order = np.random.permutation(n) if random else np.arange(n)
       improved=False
       for j in order:
           new_a, new_p, improved = coord_update(a,p,j, max_evals=?)
           if improved: a,p=new_a,new_p; improved_total=True
       if not improved: break
    return a,p
```

    best=a; bestsc=cur
    # For each lam in grid:
       h = a.copy(); h[mask]*=lam; h=normalize_nonneg(h)

       if sc<bestsc: best=h
    return best,bestsc
```

### More advanced: Use **subgradient + Bregman/entropy mirror descent**
The problem is minimize max p_t over simplex. We can use **Mirror descent** with negative entropy: update `a_j ← a_j exp(-η ∇φ_j) / Z`. This stays positive and often good for flatting. Use smooth max gradient. We can implement multiplicative step with varying η. This is similar to multiplicative gradient. It may escape local minima if η large. Combine with coordinate line search.

Implementation:
```
def entropy_step(a, temp, betas):
    p=convolve_full(a); M=p.max()
    w=softmax((p-M)/temp)
    d=direction_from_weights(w)
    dm=d - d.mean()
    scale=max(abs(dm))
    for beta in [0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5,1,2,5]:
       h=a*np.exp(-beta*dm/scale)
       h=normalize_nonneg(h)
       sc=score
```
Since a positive, no zeros; if zeros, exp protect.

### Coordinate descent implementation in detail and performance
Let's write code carefully.

```
def prepare_coord(a, p, j):
    n = a.size
    x0 = a[j]
    S0 = a.sum() - x0  # assume sum=1
    base = p.copy()
    L = np.zeros(p.size)
    Qdiag = -1
    start = j
    end = min(p.size, j + n)
    if end > start:
        t_idx = np.arange(start, end)
        k = t_idx - j
        non = k != j
        if np.any(non):
            tn = t_idx[non]
            kn = k[non]
            base[tn] -= 2.0 * x0 * a[kn]
            L[tn] = 2.0 * a[kn]
        if np.any(~non):
            Qdiag = int(t_idx[~non][0])
            base[Qdiag] -= x0*x0
    return base, L, Qdiag, S0, x0
```
Need ensure base entries not negative due to rounding; clip? `base = np.maximum(base,0)`? But if subtract contributions, rounding could negative; for ratio, small negative max maybe wrong (max likely not negative). We can `base[base<0 & base>-1e-15]=0` but if larger negative due error, clipping safe. `np.maximum(base,0)`. L nonnegative.
However if p from FFT has tiny negative for entries, base after subtract maybe negative. Clip all base to 0. 

`ratio_values(base,L,Qdiag,S0, xs)`:
```
def peak_ratio_values(base, L, Qdiag, S0, xs):
    xs = np.asarray(xs)
    num = base[None, :] + xs[:, None] * L[None, :]
    if Qdiag >= 0:
        num[:, Qdiag] = num[:, Qdiag] + xs[:, None]*xs[:, None]
    maxs = num.max(axis=1)
    denom = (S0 + xs[:, None])**2
    # denom shape K,1; use denom[:,0]
    return maxs / denom[:,0]
```
`base[None,:] + xs[:,None]*L` allocates. If K large, okay. For scalar ratio:
```
def peak_ratio_scalar(base,L,Qdiag,S0,x):
    # avoid allocation? Could do: maxs = base.max(); if Lmax>0 maxs=max(maxs, x*L_max?) But L varies with t. Since base+L*x max ≤ base.max + x*L.max but not exact. Need allocate for exact.
    num = base + x*L
    if Qdiag>=0: num[Qdiag]+=x*x
    return num.max()/((S0+x)**2)
```
Allocation per scalar golden. Could preallocate `num = base.copy()` and fill? For scalar, use `num = base + x*L` new. Fine.

Find best x:
```
def choose_x(base,L,Qdiag,S0,x0,n,cur_peak,eval_vector=30):
    if S0 <= 1e-14:
       return 1.0 if x0<=1e-14 else x0
    # upper
    mean = 1.0/n
    upper = max(1.0, 10.0*x0, 10.0*mean)
    # add peak-derived
    if cur_peak < 0.999:
       sr = np.sqrt(cur_peak+1e-15)
       if sr < 1:
          x_calc = S0*sr/(1-sr)
          if x_calc < upper*10: upper = max(upper, min(10.0, x_calc*2+1e-6))
    # sample points
    xs = [0.0, x0, S0, upper]
    # logspace
    if upper > 0:
       xs.extend(np.geomspace(upper*1e-3, upper, 20).tolist())
    if x0 > 0:
       for f in (0.1,0.25,0.5,0.75,1.25,2.0,4.0,8.0): xs.append(x0*f)
    xs = [x for x in xs if x>=0 and x<=upper*10?]
    xs = np.unique(np.clip(np.asarray(xs),0,None))
    # We can cap xs to e.g. upper*5 maybe 100
    xs = xs[xs <= 100.0]

### LP direction with p and active rows

```
def lp_directed_improve(a,p,budget,max_rows=256):
   M=p.max()
   if M<=0: return a,p,False
   rows = top_indices_by(p, max_rows)
   A = build A for rows
   for Mmult in [1.0, 1.02, 1.05]:
      try linprog maximize sum(g) s.t. A g <= Mmult*M, 0<=g<=100
      if success and sumg>1+1e-4:
          g/=sumg
          for t_mix in [0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.3,0.5,0.8]:
             h=(1-t_mix)*a + t_mix*g; h=normalize_nonneg(h); ph=score; update
   return
```

18. `prepare_mask_and_minimize(a,p,mask)`:
```
def mask_improve(a,p,mask, sample_count=30, golden_iters=20):
   m=a*mask; o=a-m
   Bs=m.sum()
   if Bs<=1e-15: return a,p,False,cur
   Q=convolution(m,m)
   L=2.0*convolution(m,o)
   base=p - L - Q  # because p = base + L + Q
   base=np.maximum(base,0)
   S0 = 1.0 - Bs
   # lambda current = 1.0
   # sample lambdas
   xs = [0.0, 1.0, S0, Bs?]; upper=100.0
   if Bs < 1e-3: upper=1000
   xs += geomspace(1e-4, upper, sample_count)
   if 1.0 > upper? no
   evaluate ratio values; best.
   if best_x close 1 and ratio>=cur: return no
   update a,p.
```

```
def lambda_ratio_values(base,L,Q,S0,Bs,lams):
   num=base[None,:]+lams[:,None]*L[None,:]+(lams[:,None]**2)*Q[None,:]
   maxs=num.max(axis=1)
   denom=(S0+lams*Bs)**2
   return maxs/denom
```

```

21. Coordinate pass. Implement `coordinate_improve_a(p, max_passes, time_limit, sample_count, golden_iters)`:
```
def coordinate_pass(a,p, use_random=True, max_passes=1, time_limit=None, sample_count=20, golden_iters=15, tol=1e-12):
  n=a.size; deadline=...; improved_total=False
  for pass in range(max_passes):
    if time exceeded: break
    order = rng.permutation(n) if use_random else np.arange(n)
    pass_improved=False
    for j in order:
      if time exceeded: break
      x0=a[j]
      # if x0 very small and others? still
      base,L,Qdiag,S0 = prepare_coord(a,p,j)
      cur_ratio=p.max()
      best_x,best_r = choose_x_coord(base,L,Qdiag,S0,x0,n,cur_ratio,sample_count,golden_iters)
      if best_r < cur_ratio*(1-tol) or abs(best_x-x0)>1e-8:
          update a,p
          pass_improved=True; improved_total=True
      # maybe if no improvement after some coords and time, continue
    if not pass_improved: break
  return a,p,improved_total
```
But coordinate pass may be too aggressive: If n=512 and each coord choose x uses 20 vector +15 scalar; 512*35=18k ratio evals; good. `choose_x_coord` robust:
```
def choose_x_coord(base, L, Qdiag, S0, x0, n, cur_peak, nsamp=20, giter=20):
    if S0 <= 1e-15:
        # function at x>0? if base max maybe 0? For one-hot, ratio=1. Return x0.
        return x0 if x0>0 else 1.0
    mean=1.0/n
    upper = max(1.0, 10.0*x0, 10.0*mean)
    if cur_peak < 0.999:
       sr=np.sqrt(cur_peak + 1e-15)
       if sr < 1.0:
          xbal = S0*sr/(1.0-sr)
          if np.isfinite(xbal): upper=max(upper, min(100.0, xbal*2+1e-6))
    xs=[0.0, x0, S0, upper]
    if upper>0:
       xs.extend(np.geomspace(upper*1e-3, upper, max(5, nsamp)).tolist())
    if x0>0:
       for fac in (0.1,0.25,0.5,0.75,1.25,2,4,8): xs.append(x0*fac)
    xs=np.asarray(xs,float)
    xs=xs[np.isfinite(xs) & (xs>=0)]
    xs=np.unique(xs)
    # cap 1000
    xs=xs[xs<=1000.0]
    if xs.size==0: xs=np.array([0.0,1.0])
    ratios=coord_ratio_values(...)
    idx=int(np.argmin(ratios)); best_x=xs[idx]; best_r=ratios[idx]
    # expand if best at max
    if xs[-1] <= 1000.0 and best_x >= xs[-1]*0.99:
       new_upper=min(1000.0, xs[-1]*5)
       xs2=np.geomspace(xs[-1], new_upper, 10)
       r2=...
       if r2.min()<best_r:
          idx2; best=...
    # golden interval from neighbors if possible
    # If best at 0 maybe interval [0, next >0]
    if xs.size>1:
       if idx==0: lo=0.0; hi=float(xs[1])
       elif idx==len(xs)-1: lo=float(xs[-2]); hi=float(xs[-1])
       else: lo=float(xs[idx-1]); hi=float(xs[idx+1])
       if hi>lo:
          scalar golden update best
    return best_x,best_r
```

def optimize_sequence(a, budget_s, rng, state):

    p=convolution_self(a)
    n=a.size

    deadline=time.monotonic()+budget_s

    # Parameter based on n
    coord_enabled = n <= 1536

    mask_moves_chunk = 200000//n  # maybe

          t_limit = min(chunk*0.5, 60)

       # mask moves

       a,p,imp,cur_score = random_mask_pass(a,p,rng,num_moves=..., time_limit=t_mask)
       if imp: no_improve=0
       else: no_improve +=1
       # gradient/LP occasionally

          a,p,imp = gradient_improve(a,p, sample_count maybe, time_limit=5)

       # if no_improve>3: perturb

          no_improve=0
          # print

```

### Testing formulas mentally
Coordinate prepare: If current `p` from convolution of normalized a. For j, base = p; valid t range j..j+n-1. For non-diag t, k=t-j. If k != j, base[t] -= 2*x0*a[k], L[t]=2*a[k]. But what if the pair (j,k) and (k,j) terms: in p[t] there are two terms: a_j a_k + a_k a_j. Removing x0 from a_j leaves a_k? base should p[t] - x0*a_k - x0*a_k = p[t] -2x0a_k. L=2a_k for new x. Good.
If k invalid? valid range ensures 0≤k<n. t from j to j+n-1. For t outside, no terms involving j. Good.
Diagonal: t=2j. p[t] has one term a_j^2. base-=x0^2, Q=1. Good.
Does `valid range` length always n? t=j to j+n-1 inclusive length n. t max 2n-2? For j=n-1, t range n-1..2n-2 length n. Good.
`t_idx=np.arange(j, j+n)` but if j+n > p.size (2n-1), use end = p.size. For j=n-1, j+n=2n-1 = p.size, end p.size, arange stop exclusive gives t up to 2n-2. Good.

Mask base via `p - L - Q`: L=2conv(m,o). For pair m_i,m_j, not in L, in Q. For m_i,o_k, conv(m,o) includes m_i o_k and also m_k o_i if indices? Since m and o disjoint, each ordered cross appears exactly once in conv(m,o)? Convolution m*o coefficient = ∑_i m_i o_{t-i}. This includes ordered pairs (i from m, k=t-i from o). The reverse (o,m) would be ∑_i o_i m_{t-i}, same set with roles swapped, and equal to m*o. The sum of ordered cross terms is m*o + o*m =2*m*o. Correct. For a fixed unordered pair (m_i,o_k), the two ordered terms are m_i o_{i+k?} and o_k m_i, both counted in 2conv. Good.
For m_i,m_j, Q=conv(m,m) includes both ordered terms if i≠j and diagonal once; matches p_mask_mask.
Thus base nonnegative.

### Mask lambda line search update
If λ=0, a_new mask zero, p_new = base/S0^2. This may improve by removing a bad block. If S0=0 (mask is all mass), λ=0 denom 0, invalid. We exclude λ=0 if S0<=0? If mask all mass, scaling to zero makes all zero invalid; but can choose small λ? If S0=0, outside sum zero, sequence entirely in mask. Scaling mask just changes scale, normalized shape same, score same. No improvement. Return no change. In `mask_improve`, if S0<=1e-15: return no improvement (or λ=1).
If Bs=0 (mask empty), no.

Use `upper = max(10.0, min(1000.0, 10.0 / max(Bs,1e-3), 10.0 / max(S0,1e-3)?))`. But if S0=0.5, Bs=0.5, 10/B=20; okay. If Bs=0.01, 10/B=1000. Good. def update_best(state, a, p=None):
   if a is None: return
   a=np.asarray(a,float).reshape(-1)
   a=np.clip(a,0,None); s=a.sum(); if s<=1e-12: return
   a=a/s
   n=a.size
   if p is None:
      p=convolution_self(a)
   else:
      p=p.astype(float); ps=p.sum(); if ps>0: p=p/ps
      p=np.maximum(p,0)
   score=float(2*n*p.max())
   if score < state['score']:
      state['score']=score; state['a']=a.copy(); state['n']=n
      # maybe official if improvement significant
```

def quad_ratio_values(base,L,Q,S0,factor,xs):

   ok=denom>1e-300

### Potential problem: `np.interp` for upsample expects xp increasing; if n=1, handle.
```
def resize_sequence(a,N):
   a=normalize_nonneg(a)
   n=a.size
   if n==1:
      return np.full(N,1.0/N)
   if n==N: return a
   if n<N:
      x_old=np.linspace(0.0,n-1,n)
      x_new=np.linspace(0.0,N-1,N)
      out=np.interp(x_new,x_old,a)
      return normalize_nonneg(out)
   else:
      cum=np.concatenate(([0.0],np.cumsum(a)))
      idx=np.linspace(0.0,n,N+1).astype(int)
      out=cum[idx[1:]]-cum[idx[:-1]]
      return normalize_nonneg(out)
```
Linear interp may create negative? no.

#### convolution function
```
def convolution(x, y=None):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if y is None:
        y = x
    else:
        y = np.asarray(y, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    m = x.size; l=y.size
    if m == 0 or l == 0:
        return np.zeros(1, dtype=np.float64)
    need = m + l - 1
    if need <= 1024:
        out = np.convolve(x, y)
    else:
        size = 1
        while size < need: size <<= 1
        fx = np.fft.rfft(x, n=size)
        fy = np.fft.rfft(y, n=size)
        out = np.fft.irfft(fx * fy, n=size)[:need]
    out = np.array(out, dtype=np.float64, copy=False)
    # For nonnegative inputs, clip small negatives; if inputs can be weights nonnegative too.
    out[np.abs(out) < 1e-15] = 0.0
    out = np.maximum(out, 0.0)
    return out
```
Clipping all negatives might alter if inputs can be negative (we don't use). Good.

But in linearized LP no convolution. In gradient weights positive. Good.

#### normalize
```
def normalize_nonneg(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        a = np.ones_like(a, dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(a.size)
    a = a / s
    return a
```

#### score/update
```
def internal_score_from_a_p(a,p): ...
```
`update_best` can use p if provided but ensure p length 2n-1.
```
def update_best(state, a, p=None):
    if a is None: return
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if a.size == 0: return
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, None)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-12: return
    a = a / s
    n = a.size
    if p is None:
        p = convolution(a,a)
    else:
        p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
        if p.size != 2*n-1:
            p = convolution(a,a)
        else:
            p = np.nan_to_num(p, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
            p = np.clip(p,0.0,None)
            ps=p.sum()
            if ps > 0: p=p/ps
    peak = float(p.max())
    score = float(2.0 * n * peak)
    if not np.isfinite(score): return
    if score < state['score'] - 1e-15:
        state['score'] = score
        state['a'] = a.copy()
        state['n'] = int(n)
        # maybe print if improved by >1e-6
        print(f"best internal score={score:.10f} n={n}", flush=True)
```
Printing every tiny improvement could be many; use a threshold flag? State can store last_print. We can add `if score < state.get('last_print', inf) - 1e-5: print; state['last_print']=score`. Initialize. Simpler: only print if improvement >1e-6.

But state initialized `{'score':inf,'a':None,'n':0,'last_print':inf}`.

#### Official score
```
def official_score(list_seq):
    try:
       val = evaluate_sequence(list_seq)
    except Exception:
       return float('inf')
    if not np.isfinite(val): return float('inf')
    return float(val)
```
No need import evaluate. It is global.

### Generic quadr minimizer
Implement `quad_ratio_values`, `quad_ratio_scalar`, `minimize_quad`.

```
def quad_ratio_values(base, L, Q, S0, factor, xs, has_Q):
    xs = np.asarray(xs, dtype=np.float64)
    denom = S0 + factor * xs
    ok = denom > 1e-300
    ratios = np.full(xs.shape, np.inf, dtype=np.float64)
    if not np.any(ok): return ratios
    num = base[None, :] + xs[:, None] * L[None, :]
    if has_Q:
        num = num + (xs[:, None] * xs[:, None]) * Q[None, :]
    maxs = num.max(axis=1)
    # Apply ok: for invalid xs set inf
    maxs = np.where(ok, maxs, np.inf)
    rat = (maxs / (denom * denom)).astype(np.float64)
    rat[~ok] = np.inf
    return rat
```
If base/L/Q large and xs large, num shape K*p_len. If K=50 p_len=4095 ->200k, okay. If K large 100 ->400k, okay.

`quad_ratio_scalar` can use values with length1 but overhead; implement direct:
```
def quad_ratio_scalar(base,L,Q,S0,factor,x,has_Q):
    denom = S0 + factor*x
    if denom <= 1e-300: return inf
    num = base + x*L
    if has_Q: num = num + x*x*Q
    mx = float(np.max(num))
    if not np.isfinite(mx): return inf
    return mx/(denom*denom)
```
This allocates num each scalar. In golden, maybe okay.

`minimize_quad`:

def minimize_quad(base,L,Q,S0,factor,cur_x,upper_hint,cur_peak,sample_count=24,golden_iters=20):

    cpx = 1 if np.isfinite(cur_x) else 0.0
    upper = max(1.0, 10.0*max(0.0,cpx), 10.0*max(1e-12, upper_hint), 10.0/max(factor,1e-6)? maybe cap)
```

    upper = min(1000.0, max(1.0, 10.0*max(0.0,cur_x), 10.0*upper_hint, 10.0/max(factor,1e-6)))
```

if sr<1:
    xbal = S0*sr/(max(1e-9, factor*(1-sr)))
    upper=max(upper, min(1000, xbal*2+1e-6))
```

    pts = [0.0, cur_x, upper]
    if S0 > 0 and factor > 0:
        pts.append(S0/factor)  # equal sums
        pts.append(10*S0/factor)
    if cur_x > 0:
        for f in (0.1,0.25,0.5,0.75,1.25,2,4,8): pts.append(cur_x*f)
    if upper > 0:
        pts.extend(np.geomspace(upper*1e-4, upper, max(6, sample_count)).tolist())
    pts = [t for t in pts if np.isfinite(t) and t>=0 and t<=1000]
    xs = np.unique(np.asarray(pts))
    if xs.size==0: xs=np.array([0.0,1.0])
    ratios = quad_ratio_values(...)
    idx = int(np.argmin(ratios)); best_x=xs[idx]; best_r=ratios[idx]
    # expansion
    if best_x >= xs[-1]*0.99 and xs[-1] < 1000:
       new_upper = min(1000.0, xs[-1]*5.0)
       xs2 = np.geomspace(xs[-1], new_upper, 10)
       r2=quad_ratio_values(...)
       if np.any(np.isfinite(r2)):
          i2=argmin; if r2[i2]<best_r: best...
    # golden
    if xs.size>1:
       # find neighbors around best_x in original xs (not expanded) maybe use xs sorted.
       idx_pos = int(np.searchsorted(xs, best_x))
       lo=0 if idx_pos==0 else max(0.0, xs[idx_pos-1])
       hi=best_x if idx_pos>0 else (xs[1] if len>1 else upper*0.1)
       # Actually if best at idx0, we want [0, xs[1]]. if best at last, [xs[-2], xs[-1]].
       # Use easier:
       # find closed index in xs to best
       ci = int(np.argmin(np.abs(xs-best_x)))
       if len(xs)>1:
          if ci==0: lo=0.0; hi=float(xs[1])
          elif ci==len(xs)-1: lo=float(xs[-2]); hi=float(xs[-1])
          else: lo=float(xs[ci-1]); hi=float(xs[ci+1])
          if hi>lo:
             # golden
             for _ in range(golden_iters): ...
      # also maybe golden over [0,upper] if sample best poor? Not.
    return best_x
```
If `best_r` inf (S0=0 and xs=0 invalid), choose a positive point. In samples include 1.0; ok.
Golden update:
```
gr = (np.sqrt(5.0)-1.0)/2.0
c = hi - gr*(hi-lo); d = lo + gr*(hi-lo)
fc=quad_ratio_scalar(...,c); fd=...
if fc<fd: best if fc<best_r...
for _ in range(golden_iters): ...
```

#### Update from minimize_quad for coord/mask
After best_x return, compute ratio best_r maybe by scalar to compare. In functions, we can call `minimize_quad` returns x only; caller computes ratio? We can have minimize return x,best_r. Let's return both for efficiency (best_r from scalar maybe). It can keep best_r from sample/golden. Good.

`minimize_quad` returns `(best_x, best_r)`.

Generic update:
```
def apply_quad_update(a,p, base,L,Q, S0, factor, x_new, is_mask, mask=None):
    denom = S0 + factor*x_new
    if denom <= 1e-300: return a,p,False
    scale = 1.0/denom
    if is_mask:
       a_new = a * scale
       if mask is not None:
          a_new[mask] *= x_new
    else:
       # coordinate? Need mask bool single for simplicity. We can pass mask.
       a_new = a * scale
       if mask is not None:
          a_new[mask] *= x_new
    # p_new = (base + x*L + x^2 Q)*scale^2
    p_un = base + x_new*L
    if np.any(Q): p_un = p_un + (x_new*x_new)*Q
    p_new = p_un * (scale*scale)
    a_new = normalize_nonneg(a_new)
    p_new = np.nan_to_num(p_new, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    p_new = np.clip(p_new,0.0,None)
    ps = float(p_new.sum())
    if ps > 1e-300: p_new = p_new / ps
    return a_new,p_new,True
```
For coord, base/L/Q prepared from current p; factor=1, x_new is new value. For mask, factor=Bs, x_new=λ. A_new formula works: a_new = (a/scale? wait a*scale then a_new[mask]*=x_new). For outside: a*scale. For inside: a*λ*scale. Since scale=1/(S0+λBs). Good.
For `a_new[mask] *= x_new`, if x_new=0, zeros.
Need ensure mask integer? Boolean array.
For coordinate, mask can be boolean with one True. Good.

But in coordinate prepare, base/L/Q are relative to old coordinate value; if x_new is new absolute value, factor=1. For `a_new = a*scale; a_new[mask] *= x_new`? Let's verify: a old sum1, x0=a[j]. We want outside old a_i for i≠j scaled by 1/(S0+x_new). Inside new x_new/(S0+x_new). `a_new = a*scale; a_new[j] = x_new*scale`. If we do `a_new = a*scale; a_new[mask] *= x_new`, for inside: a_old_j*scale*x_new = x0*x_new*scale, wrong! The factor scales old value. For mask scaling, inside old value multiplied by λ relative to old, so `a_old*λ*scale`. For coordinate, parameter x_new is absolute value, not λ. We need separate update:
- Coord: outside a_i*scale (scale=1/(S0+x_new)), inside x_new*scale.
- Mask: inside a_i*λ*scale (scale=1/(S0+λBs)), outside a_i*scale.
So `apply_quad_update` different.
For mask, can use `a_new = a*scale; a_new[mask] = a[mask]*λ*scale` or `a_new = (a + a*(λ-1)*mask) * scale`? For coord, with λ = x_new/x0 if x0>0; if x0=0, different. Simpler separate functions or pass mode:
```
if mode == 'coord':
   a_new = a * scale
   a_new[j] = x_new * scale
else: # mask
   a_new = a * scale
   a_new[mask] = a[mask] * x_new * scale
```
For mask base/L/Q factor=Bs, x_new=λ. Good.
For coordinate, factor=1, x_new=x. p_new formula same.

def coord_improve_one(a,p,j,sample_count=...,golden=...):
    n=a.size; x0=a[j]; S0=1-x0
    base=p.copy(); L=zeros; Q=zeros
    start=j; end=min(p.size,j+n)
    if end>start:
      t_idx=np.arange(start,end)
      k=t_idx-j
      non=k!=j
      if np.any(non): ...
      diag=np.where(k==j)
      if any: q=int(t_idx[diag][0]); Q[q]=1; base[q]-=x0*x0
    base=np.maximum(base,0)
    upper_hint=1.0/n
    cur_peak=p.max()
    x_best,r_best=minimize_quad(base,L,Q,S0,1.0,x0,upper_hint,cur_peak,sample_count,golden)
    scale=1/(S0+x_best)
    a_new=a*scale; a_new[j]=x_best*scale
    p_un=base + x_best*L + (x_best*x_best)*Q
    p_new=p_un*scale*scale
    a_new=normalize_nonneg(a_new); p_new=... normalize
    return a_new,p_new,(r_best < cur_peak)
```
If S0+x_best near 0 (x_best=0,S0=0), invalid. If one-hot, S0=0, x0=1; minimize_quad should return x0? In upper with S0=0, samples 0 invalid, 1 valid; ratio maybe 1. Return x0. scale=1. good.
Set mask not needed.
Only accept if new_peak = p_new.max() < cur_peak*(1-1e-12). Use p_new after normalization, not r_best (may mismatch due clipping). We can compute `new_peak = float(p_new.max())` and accept if < cur_peak*(1-1e-12). Good.

#### `mask_improve_one`
```
def mask_improve_one(a,p,mask,sample_count=...,golden=...):
    m = np.where(mask, a, 0.0)
    Bs = float(m.sum())
    S0 = 1.0 - Bs
    if Bs <=1e-15 or S0 <=1e-15: return a,p,False
    o = a - m
    Q = convolution(m,m)
    L = 2.0 * convolution(m,o)
    base = p - L - Q
    base = np.nan_to_num(base, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    base = np.clip(base,0.0,None)
    cur_peak=p.max()
    lambda_best, r_best = minimize_quad(base,L,Q,S0,Bs,1.0,1.0,cur_peak,sample_count,golden)
    denom=S0+Bs*lambda_best
    if denom <=1e-300: no
    scale=1/denom
    a_new=a*scale
    a_new[mask]=a[mask]*lambda_best*scale
    p_un=base + lambda_best*L + (lambda_best*lambda_best)*Q
    p_new=p_un*scale*scale
    a_new=normalize_nonneg(a_new); p_new=...
    new_peak=p_new.max()
    if new_peak < cur_peak*(1-1e-12): return a_new,p_new,True
```
`a_new[mask]=a[mask]*lambda_best*scale`: if mask boolean, ok. Note `a_new[mask] =` uses mask indexing. Ensure mask bool.

`prepare_mask` maybe base from p and L/Q. Use convolution for Q and L. If p drift, base clip. 

### Linearized LP function
```
def linearized_lp_improve(a,p,row_count=None,time_limit=None):
    if linprog is None: return a,p,False
    n=a.size; m=a.size*2-1
    if n>1536: return
    M=float(p.max())
    if M <=0: return
    if row_count is None: row_count=m if m<=700 else 512
    # rows top by p
    if row_count >= m: rows=np.arange(m)
    else: rows=np.argpartition(p,m-row_count)[-row_count:]
    rows = np.sort(rows) # maybe not needed
    B = np.zeros((rows.size,n))
    for r,t in enumerate(rows):
       lo=max(0,t-n+1); hi=min(n,t+1)
       if hi>lo:
           idx=np.arange(lo,hi)
           B[r, idx] = 2.0 * a[t-idx]
    A_ub = np.empty((B.shape[0], n+1))
    A_ub[:,:n]=B
    A_ub[:,n]=-1.0
    b_ub = p[rows].astype(float)
    c = np.zeros(n+1); c[n]=1.0
    A_eq = np.zeros((1,n+1)); A_eq[0,:n]=1.0
    b_eq = np.array([1.0])
    bounds = [(0.0,1.0)]*n + [(0.0, max(1.0, 10.0*M))]
    try:
       res=linprog(c, A_ub=A_ub, b_ub=b_ub, A_eq=A_eq, b_eq=b_eq, bounds=bounds, method='highs')
    except Exception: return
    if res.status !=0: return
    cand = res.x[:n]
    cand = normalize_nonneg(cand)
    # line search mix
    cur_peak=M; best=a; best_p=p; best_peak=M
    for alpha in (0.05,0.1,0.2,0.3,0.5,0.7,1.0):
        h=(1-alpha)*a + alpha*cand
        h=normalize_nonneg(h)
        ph=convolution(h,h).max()
        if ph < best_peak*(1-1e-12): best=h; best_p=...
    if best_peak < M: return best,best_p,True
```
Building B for rows large; for all rows n=512, loops 1023 with idx arrays, okay. Could vectorize but fine.
Need update best_p if using candidate; use convolution. 
If LP candidate itself good.
We can try row_count multiple? one call.

def perturb_sequence(a,p,rng,scale):
   h = a * np.exp(scale*rng.normal(0,1,a.size))

### local_search function
Let's write with state updates.
```
def local_search(a, p, budget_s, rng, state, use_lp=True):
    a=normalize_nonneg(a); p=convolution(a,a); n=a.size
    update_best(state,a,p)
    deadline=time.monotonic()+budget_s
    no_improve=0
    moves_since_recompute=0
    loop=0
    coord_reduce = ... based on n
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        loop +=1
        # recompute periodically
        if moves_since_recompute >= 30:
            a=normalize_nonneg(a); p=convolution(a,a); moves_since_recompute=0
        cur_score=2*n*p.max()
        # coordinate pass if n<=1536 and (loop % 2 == 1 or no_improve>2)
        if n <= 1200 and time.monotonic() < deadline - 5:
            tcoord = min(20.0, max(2.0, (deadline-time.monotonic())*0.1))
            if n <= 600: tcoord = min(30.0, tcoord)
            a,p,imp = coordinate_pass(a,p,rng,max_passes=1,time_limit=tcoord, sample_count=18 if n<1024 else 12, golden_iters=14 if n<1024 else 10)
            moves_since_recompute += 5
            if imp: no_improve=0; update_best(state,a,p)
            else: no_improve += 1
        # mask moves
        tmask = min(20.0, max(2.0, (deadline-time.monotonic())*0.2))
        num_moves = max(10, int(tmask * (800000 // max(1,n))))  # approx? maybe simpler 100 for 512
        num_moves = max(20, min(500, 200000//max(1,n)))
        a,p,imp = random_mask_pass(a,p,rng,num_moves,num_moves,time_limit=tmask)
        moves_since_recompute += num_moves
        if imp: no_improve=0; update_best(state,a,p)
        else: no_improve +=1
        # gradient every 3 loops
        if loop % 3 == 0 and time.monotonic() < deadline - 5:
            a,p,imp = gradient_improve(a,p,betas=(...))
            if imp: no_improve=0; update_best
            else: no_improve+=1
        # LP every 5 loops
        if use_lp and loop % 5 ==0 and n<=1024 and time.monotonic() < deadline - 10:
            a,p,imp = linearized_lp_improve(...)
            if imp: no_improve=0; update_best
            else:
               a,p,imp = fstar_lp_improve(...)
               ...
        # if stuck perturb
        if no_improve >= 4 and time.monotonic() < deadline - 5:
            scale = 0.05 + 0.05*min(5,no_improve//2)
            a,p = perturb_sequence(a,p,rng,scale)
            no_improve = 0
            update_best(state,a,p) # if perturbation worse not update best but current changed
        # check state best maybe
    update_best(state,a,p)
    return state['a'], state['score']
```
Need avoid no_improve increment too aggressively; mask pass may improve sometimes. Fine.
`random_mask_pass` should update state if improved significantly; but local loop updates after return. If many small improvements within pass, p current improves. State updated at end. okay.

`coordinate_pass` returns `imp` true if any coordinate improved. It updates a,p. It can use `update_best` internally? To track best during pass, pass state? Simpler no, local updates after pass. But if coordinate pass long and best improves, state not updated until end; okay.
Need ensure local_search current `a` is not lost if no improvement; it may perturb. Best stored separately.

def random_mask_pass(a,p,rng,num_moves,time_limit):

`choose_mask`:
```
def choose_mask(a,p,rng,n):
   mode = int(rng.integers(0,6))
   mask=np.zeros(n,bool)
   if mode==0:
      # active peak segment
      t = int(np.argmax(p))
      lo=max(0,t-n+1); hi=min(n,t+1)
      mask[lo:hi]=True
   elif mode==1:
      # second active peak random top t
      t = int(rng.choice(np.argsort(p)[-max(1,n//10):])) maybe expensive sort each? use top? simple.
   elif mode==2:
      # top a
      k=max(1,min(n,n//5))
      idx=np.argpartition(a, -k)[-k:]
      mask[idx]=True
   elif mode==3:
      # bottom a
      k=max(1,min(n,n//5))
      idx=np.argpartition(a, k)[:k]
      mask[idx]=True
   elif mode==4:
      # contiguous random
      length=int(rng.integers(max(1,n//20), max(2,n//2)+1))
      start=int(rng.integers(0, max(1,n-length+1)))
      mask[start:start+length]=True
   else:
      pprob=float(rng.uniform(0.1,0.4))
      mask=rng.random(n)<pprob
   # if mask sum too small/large, adjust? not necessary
   if not np.any(mask): mask[0]=True
   if mask.sum() == n: mask[0]=False # avoid whole
   return mask
```
`np.argpartition(a, -k)` with k=0? handle. For random top p, avoid sort each time; we can use `int(rng.integers(n))` for active? To target peaks, precompute top peaks? In local loop, p changes. Could do `top_count=max(5,n//20); idx=np.argpartition(p, n-top_count)[-top_count:]; t=idx[rng.integers(top_count)]`. This is O(n) per mask, okay. But doing per mask expensive. We can select top_count once per pass? Simpler use mode0 argmax O(1) (p.argmax), mode1 random coordinate. Active peaks concentrated near argmax often. Good.
For mode1: `t=int(rng.integers(n))` or top p? maybe random. Fine.

We can also scale down active segment; but exact λ may choose. For top a mask, likely λ<1. 

### Coordinate pass function with time
```
def coordinate_pass(a,p,rng,max_passes=1,time_limit=20.0,sample_count=18,golden_iters=14):
   n=a.size; start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+time_limit
   improved_total=False
   for pass_idx in range(max_passes):
      if time.monotonic()>=deadline: break
      order = rng.permutation(n) if rng is not None else np.arange(n)
      pass_improved=False
      consec_no=0
      for j in order:
         if time.monotonic()>=deadline: break
         if consec_no > 50 and n>1024: break? Maybe not.
         x0=a[j]
         # Quick check if x0 very small and p peak not likely? still.
         a_new,p_new,imp = coord_improve_one(a,p,j,sample_count,golden_iters)
         if imp:
            a,p=a_new,p_new; pass_improved=True; improved_total=True; consec_no=0
         else:
            consec_no+=1
      if not pass_improved: break
   return a,p,improved_total
```
50 consecutive no improvement but later may; for time limit, okay. We can not break, but if time short.

### fstar LP matrix build performance
Build A for rows with loops. Use B half? We can write `build_conv_matrix(a, rows)` returns A rows a[t-j]. Use in both LPs.
```
def build_conv_matrix(a, rows):
   n=a.size; A=np.zeros((len(rows),n))
   for r,t in enumerate(np.asarray(rows,dtype=int)):
      lo=max(0,t-n+1); hi=min(n,t+1)
      if hi>lo:
         idx=np.arange(lo,hi)
         A[r, hi?]=a[t-idx]
   return A
```
Linearized LP uses 2*A.

`linearized_lp_improve` build B = 2*build_conv_matrix(a, rows). 
`fstar` uses A.

```python
"""
Search for low-score nonnegative step-function coefficients.

The score is
    2 n max_t (a*a)_t / (sum a)^2,
where a*a is the full linear convolution.  The search normalizes sequences to
sum 1 and minimizes the peak convolution coefficient.  It combines:
  - coordinate-wise exact 1-D minimization,
  - exact group-scaling line searches over random/structured masks,
  - block sweeps,
  - smooth-max gradient steps,
  - linearized LP moves,
  - the classic covering LP direction,
  - multi-length continuation and random restarts.
"""

import numpy as np
import time

try:
    from scipy.optimize import linprog
except Exception:
    linprog = None


def normalize_nonneg(a):
    """Clip to nonnegative values and normalize to sum 1."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        a = np.ones_like(a, dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(a.size)
    return a / s


def convolution(x, y=None):
    """Full convolution of two nonnegative vectors; FFT for larger sizes."""
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if y is None:
        y = x
    else:
        y = np.asarray(y, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    m = x.size
    l = y.size
    if m == 0 or l == 0:
        return np.zeros(1, dtype=np.float64)
    need = m + l - 1
    if need <= 1024:
        out = np.convolve(x, y)
    else:
        size = 1
        while size < need:
            size <<= 1
        fx = np.fft.rfft(x, n=size)
        fy = np.fft.rfft(y, n=size)
        out = np.fft.irfft(fx * fy, n=size)[:need]
    out = np.array(out, dtype=np.float64, copy=False)
    out = np.nan_to_num(out, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    out = np.maximum(out, 0.0)
    return out


def internal_score(a):
    """Exact internal objective for a (not necessarily normalized) sequence."""
    a = normalize_nonneg(a)
    n = a.size
    p = convolution(a, a)
    return float(2.0 * n * float(p.max()))


def update_best(state, a, p=None):
    """Update global best state dictionary if score improves."""
    if a is None:
        return
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if a.size == 0:
        return
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return
    a = a / s
    n = a.size
    if p is None:
        p = convolution(a, a)
    else:
        p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
        if p.size != 2 * n - 1:
            p = convolution(a, a)
        else:
            p = np.nan_to_num(p, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
            p = np.clip(p, 0.0, None)
            ps = float(p.sum())
            if ps > 0.0:
                p = p / ps
    score = float(2.0 * n * float(p.max()))
    if not np.isfinite(score):
        return
    if score < state["score"] - 1e-15:
        last = state.get("last_print", float("inf"))
        state["score"] = score
        state["a"] = a.copy()
        state["n"] = int(n)
        if score < last - 5e-6:
            print(f"best internal score={score:.10f} n={n}", flush=True)
            state["last_print"] = score


def official_score(seq):
    """Call the provided evaluator safely."""
    try:
        val = evaluate_sequence(seq)
    except Exception:
        return float("inf")
    if not np.isfinite(val):
        return float("inf")
    return float(val)


def resize_sequence(a, N):
    """Resize a nonnegative sequence to length N while preserving mass."""
    a = normalize_nonneg(a)
    n = a.size
    N = int(max(2, N))
    if n == N:
        return a
    if n == 0:
        return np.ones(N, dtype=np.float64) / N
    if n == 1:
        return np.ones(N, dtype=np.float64) / N
    if n < N:
        x_old = np.linspace(0.0, n - 1, n)
        x_new = np.linspace(0.0, N - 1, N)
        out = np.interp(x_new, x_old, a)
        return normalize_nonneg(out)
    cum = np.concatenate(([0.0], np.cumsum(a)))
    idx = np.linspace(0.0, n, N + 1).astype(np.int64)
    out = cum[idx[1:]] - cum[idx[:-1]]
    return normalize_nonneg(out)


def add_candidate(lst, a):
    """Evaluate and append a candidate sequence to a list of tuples."""
    a = normalize_nonneg(a)
    p = convolution(a, a)
    sc = float(2.0 * a.size * float(p.max()))
    lst.append((sc, a, p))


def make_block_pattern(N, K, rng):
    """Random constant-height block pattern."""
    N = int(max(2, N))
    K = int(max(1, min(max(1, int(K)), N)))
    lens = rng.dirichlet(np.ones(K)) * N
    lens = np.floor(lens).astype(np.int64)
    lens = np.maximum(1, lens)
    while lens.sum() < N:
        lens[int(rng.integers(K))] += 1
    while lens.sum() > N:
        i = int(np.argmax(lens))
        if lens[i] > 1:
            lens[i] -= 1
        else:
            break
    masses = rng.dirichlet(np.ones(K))
    out = np.zeros(N, dtype=np.float64)
    pos = 0
    for L, m in zip(lens, masses):
        L = int(L)
        if L > 0 and pos + L <= N:
            out[pos:pos + L] = float(m) / L
            pos += L
        elif L > 0:
            out[pos:N] = float(m) / max(1, N - pos)
            break
    return normalize_nonneg(out)


def make_wave_pattern(N, rng):
    """Nonnegative sinusoidal pattern."""
    N = int(max(2, N))
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, N)
    f1 = int(rng.integers(1, 7))
    f2 = int(rng.integers(2, 10))
    a1 = float(rng.uniform(0.25, 1.0))
    a2 = float(rng.uniform(0.05, 0.55))
    p1 = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
    p2 = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
    y = 1.0 + a1 * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * f1 * x + p1) + a2 * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * f2 * x + p2)
    y = np.maximum(0.0, y)
    if float(y.sum()) <= 1e-12:
        y = 1.0 + 0.7 * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * f1 * x)
    return normalize_nonneg(y)


def make_smooth_noise(N, rng):
    """Smoothed random positive pattern."""
    N = int(max(2, N))
    y = rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, size=N)
    wlen = max(1, min(N, 1 + N // 80))
    if wlen > 1:
        w = np.ones(wlen, dtype=np.float64)
        w /= w.sum()
        y = np.convolve(y, w, mode="same")
    out = np.exp(0.65 * y)
    return normalize_nonneg(out)


def top_rows(p, K):
    """Indices of largest K values of p."""
    m = p.size
    K = int(max(1, min(K, m)))
    if K == m:
        return np.arange(m, dtype=np.int64)
    return np.argpartition(p, m - K)[-K:].astype(np.int64)


def softmax_weights(p, temp):
    """Softmax weights over convolution peaks."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    M = float(p.max())
    if not np.isfinite(M) or M <= 0.0:
        w = np.zeros_like(p)
        w[int(np.argmax(p))] = 1.0
        return w
    if temp <= 1e-300:
        w = np.zeros_like(p)
        top = int(np.argmax(p))
        w[top] = 1.0
        return w
    e = (p - M) / temp
    e = np.clip(e, -700.0, 0.0)
    w = np.exp(e)
    s = float(w.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        w = np.zeros_like(p)
        w[int(np.argmax(p))] = 1.0
    else:
        w /= s
    return w


def topk_weights(p, K):
    """Uniform weights over the K largest peaks."""
    m = p.size
    K = int(max(1, min(K, m)))
    w = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
    if K == m:
        w.fill(1.0 / m)
    else:
        idx = np.argpartition(p, m - K)[-K:]
        w[idx] = 1.0 / K
    return w


def gradient_from_weights(a, w):
    """Gradient direction D_j = 2 sum_t w_t a_{t-j}."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    conv = convolution(a, w[::-1])
    idx = (2 * n - 2) - np.arange(n, dtype=np.int64)
    return 2.0 * conv[idx]


def quad_ratio_values(base, L, Q, S0, factor, xs, has_Q):
    """Values of max_t(base+xs L+xs^2 Q)/(S0+factor*xs)^2."""
    xs = np.asarray(xs, dtype=np.float64)
    if xs.size == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    denom = S0 + factor * xs
    ok = denom > 1e-300
    ratios = np.full(xs.shape, np.inf, dtype=np.float64)
    if not np.any(ok):
        return ratios
    num = base[None, :] + xs[:, None] * L[None, :]
    if has_Q:
        num = num + (xs[:, None] * xs[:, None]) * Q[None, :]
    maxs = num.max(axis=1)
    safe = np.where(ok, denom, 1.0)
    ratios = maxs / (safe * safe)
    ratios = np.where(ok, ratios, np.inf)
    return ratios


def quad_ratio_scalar(base, L, Q, S0, factor, x, has_Q):
    """Scalar version of quad_ratio_values."""
    denom = S0 + factor * x
    if denom <= 1e-300:
        return float("inf")
    num = base + x * L
    if has_Q:
        num = num + (x * x) * Q
    mx = float(np.max(num))
    if not np.isfinite(mx):
        return float("inf")
    return mx / (denom * denom)


def minimize_quad(base, L, Q, S0, factor, cur_x, upper_hint, cur_peak,
                  sample_count=24, golden_iters=20):
    """
    Minimize max_t(base + x L + x^2 Q) / (S0 + factor x)^2 over x >= 0.
    Returns (x, value).
    """
    if S0 <= 1e-15 and factor <= 1e-15:
        cur = cur_x if np.isfinite(cur_x) and cur_x > 0.0 else 1.0
        return cur, quad_ratio_scalar(base, L, Q, S0, factor, cur, np.any(Q > 0))

    cur = float(cur_x) if np.isfinite(cur_x) and cur_x > 0.0 else 1.0
    upper = max(1.0, 10.0 * cur, 10.0 * max(1e-12, float(upper_hint)),
                10.0 / max(factor, 1e-6))
    if cur_peak < 0.999 and cur_peak > 0.0:
        sr = float(np.sqrt(min(0.999, max(1e-12, cur_peak))))
        den = factor * max(1e-9, 1.0 - sr)
        if den > 0.0:
            xbal = S0 * sr / den
            if np.isfinite(xbal) and xbal > 0.0:
                upper = max(upper, min(1000.0, 2.0 * xbal + 1e-6))
    upper = min(1000.0, upper)

    pts = [0.0, cur, 1.0, S0 / max(factor, 1e-12), 10.0 * S0 / max(factor, 1e-12), upper]
    for f in (0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1.25, 2.0, 4.0, 8.0):
        pts.append(cur * f)
    if upper > 0.0:
        pts.extend(np.geomspace(max(upper * 1e-4, 1e-8), upper, max(6, int(sample_count))).tolist())

    xs = np.asarray(pts, dtype=np.float64)
    xs = xs[np.isfinite(xs) & (xs >= 0.0) & (xs <= 1000.0)]
    xs = np.unique(xs)
    if xs.size == 0:
        xs = np.array([0.0, 1.0], dtype=np.float64)

    has_Q = bool(np.any(Q > 0))
    ratios = quad_ratio_values(base, L, Q, S0, factor, xs, has_Q)
    finite = np.isfinite(ratios)
    if not np.any(finite):
        return cur, quad_ratio_scalar(base, L, Q, S0, factor, cur, has_Q)

    idx = int(np.argmax(ratios * np.where(finite, 1.0, -np.inf)))
    best_x = float(xs[idx])
    best_r = float(ratios[idx])

    # Expand if best is near current upper endpoint.
    if best_x >= xs[-1] * 0.99 and xs[-1] < 1000.0:
        new_upper = min(1000.0, xs[-1] * 5.0)
        xs2 = np.geomspace(xs[-1], new_upper, 10)
        r2 = quad_ratio_values(base, L, Q, S0, factor, xs2, has_Q)
        fi2 = np.isfinite(r2)
        if np.any(fi2):
            i2 = int(np.argmax(r2 * np.where(fi2, 1.0, -np.inf)))
            if r2[i2] < best_r:
                best_x = float(xs2[i2])
                best_r = float(r2[i2])
                xs = np.concatenate([xs, xs2])
                xs = np.unique(np.sort(xs))

    # Local golden-section refinement near best sample point.
    if xs.size >= 2:
        ci = int(np.argmin(np.abs(xs - best_x)))
        if ci == 0:
            lo = 0.0
            hi = float(xs[1])
        elif ci == xs.size - 1:
            lo = float(xs[-2])
            hi = float(xs[-1])
        else:
            lo = float(xs[ci - 1])
            hi = float(xs[ci + 1])
        if hi > lo and hi > 0.0:
            gr = (np.sqrt(5.0) - 1.0) / 2.0
            c = hi - gr * (hi - lo)
            d = lo + gr * (hi - lo)
            fc = quad_ratio_scalar(base, L, Q, S0, factor, c, has_Q)
            fd = quad_ratio_scalar(base, L, Q, S0, factor, d, has_Q)
            if fc < best_r:
                best_r = fc
                best_x = c
            if fd < best_r:
                best_r = fd
                best_x = d

            for _ in range(max(0, int(golden_iters))):
                if hi - lo <= 1e-14:
                    break
                if not np.isfinite(fc) or not np.isfinite(fd):
                    break
                if fc < fd:
                    hi = d
                    d = c
                    fd = fc
                    c = hi - gr * (hi - lo)
                    fc = quad_ratio_scalar(base, L, Q, S0, factor, c, has_Q)
                else:
                    lo = c
                    c = d
                    fc = fd
                    d = lo + gr * (hi - lo)
                    fd = quad_ratio_scalar(base, L, Q, S0, factor, d, has_Q)
                if fc < best_r:
                    best_r = fc
                    best_x = c
                if fd < best_r:
                    best_r = fd
                    best_x = d
    return best_x, float(best_r)


def coord_improve_one(a, p, j, sample_count=20, golden_iters=14):
    """Exact one-coordinate minimization. Returns (a_new,p_new,improved)."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    x0 = float(a[j])
    S0 = 1.0 - x0
    if S0 < 0.0:
        S0 = 0.0

    base = p.copy()
    L = np.zeros(p.size, dtype=np.float64)
    Q = np.zeros(p.size, dtype=np.float64)

    start = j
    end = min(p.size, j + n)
    if end > start:
        t_idx = np.arange(start, end, dtype=np.int64)
        k = t_idx - j
        non = k != j
        if np.any(non):
            tn = t_idx[non]
            kn = k[non]
            base[tn] -= 2.0 * x0 * a[kn]
            L[tn] = 2.0 * a[kn]
        if np.any(~non):
            qpos = int(t_idx[~non][0])
            base[qpos] -= x0 * x0
            Q[qpos] = 1.0

    base = np.nan_to_num(base, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    base = np.clip(base, 0.0, None)

    cur_peak = float(p.max())
    x_best, _ = minimize_quad(
        base, L, Q, S0, 1.0, x0, 1.0 / max(1, n), cur_peak,
        sample_count=max(6, int(sample_count)),
        golden_iters=max(4, int(golden_iters))
    )

    denom = S0 + x_best
    if denom <= 1e-300:
        return a, p, False

    scale = 1.0 / denom
    a_new = a * scale
    a_new[j] = x_best * scale

    p_un = base + x_best * L
    if np.any(Q > 0):
        p_un = p_un + (x_best * x_best) * Q
    p_new = p_un * (scale * scale)

    a_new = normalize_nonneg(a_new)
    p_new = np.nan_to_num(p_new, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    p_new = np.clip(p_new, 0.0, None)
    ps = float(p_new.sum())
    if ps > 1e-300:
        p_new = p_new / ps

    improved = float(p_new.max()) < cur_peak * (1.0 - 1e-12)
    return a_new, p_new, improved


def coordinate_pass(a, p, rng, max_passes=1, time_limit=20.0,
                    sample_count=18, golden_iters=12):
    """Run passes of exact coordinate descent."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    start_time = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start_time + max(0.0, float(time_limit))
    improved_total = False

    for _pass in range(max(1, int(max_passes))):
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
            break
        if rng is None:
            order = np.arange(n)
        else:
            order = rng.permutation(n)
        pass_improved = False
        for j in order:
            if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
                break
            a, p, imp = coord_improve_one(
                a, p, int(j),
                sample_count=sample_count,
                golden_iters=golden_iters
            )
            if imp:
                pass_improved = True
                improved_total = True
        if not pass_improved:
            break
    return a, p, improved_total


def mask_improve_one(a, p, mask, sample_count=18, golden_iters=12):
    """
    Exact 1-D line search for scaling a mask:
       a -> normalize(a with a[mask] multiplied by lambda)
    Returns (a_new,p_new,improved).
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    mask = np.asarray(mask, dtype=bool).reshape(-1)
    n = a.size
    if mask.size != n:
        return a, p, False
    if not np.any(mask) or mask.size <= 0:
        return a, p, False
    if mask.size == n:
        return a, p, False

    m = np.where(mask, a, 0.0)
    Bs = float(m.sum())
    S0 = 1.0 - Bs
    if Bs <= 1e-15 or S0 <= 1e-15:
        return a, p, False

    o = a - m
    Q = convolution(m, m)
    L = 2.0 * convolution(m, o)
    base = p - L - Q
    base = np.nan_to_num(base, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    base = np.clip(base, 0.0, None)

    cur_peak = float(p.max())
    lam_best, _ = minimize_quad(
        base, L, Q, S0, Bs, 1.0, 1.0, cur_peak,
        sample_count=max(6, int(sample_count)),
        golden_iters=max(4, int(golden_iters))
    )

    denom = S0 + Bs * lam_best
    if denom <= 1e-300:
        return a, p, False

    scale = 1.0 / denom
    a_new = a * scale
    a_new[mask] = a[mask] * (lam_best * scale)

    p_un = base + lam_best * L
    p_un = p_un + (lam_best * lam_best) * Q
    p_new = p_un * (scale * scale)

    a_new = normalize_nonneg(a_new)
    p_new = np.nan_to_num(p_new, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    p_new = np.clip(p_new, 0.0, None)
    ps = float(p_new.sum())
    if ps > 1e-300:
        p_new = p_new / ps

    improved = float(p_new.max()) < cur_peak * (1.0 - 1e-12)
    return a_new, p_new, improved


def choose_mask(a, p, rng, n):
    """Pick a useful mask for group scaling."""
    mask = np.zeros(n, dtype=bool)
    mode = int(rng.integers(0, 7))
    if mode == 0:
        # Segment involved in the current largest peak.
        t = int(np.argmax(p))
        lo = max(0, t - n + 1)
        hi = min(n, t + 1)
        mask[lo:hi] = True
    elif mode == 1:
        # Segment involving a random high peak.
        k = max(1, min(n // 20, 64))
        idx = np.argpartition(p, -k)[-k:]
        t = int(idx[int(rng.integers(k))])
        lo = max(0, t - n + 1)
        hi = min(n, t + 1)
        mask[lo:hi] = True
    elif mode == 2:
        # Coordinates with largest values.
        k = max(1, min(n // 5, 128))
        idx = np.argpartition(a, -k)[-k:]
        mask[idx] = True
    elif mode == 3:
        # Coordinates with smallest values.
        k = max(1, min(n // 5, 128))
        idx = np.argpartition(a, k)[:k]
        mask[idx] = True
    elif mode == 4:
        # Random contiguous block.
        length = int(rng.integers(max(1, n // 30), max(2, n // 2) + 1))
        length = min(length, n)
        start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - length + 1)))
        mask[start:start + length] = True
    elif mode == 5:
        # Strided mask.
        stride = int(rng.integers(2, 11))
        width = max(1, int(stride * float(rng.uniform(0.15, 0.55))))
        width = min(width, stride)
        mask[(np.arange(n) % stride) < width] = True
    else:
        # Random subset.
        prob = float(rng.uniform(0.08, 0.45))
        mask = rng.random(n) < prob

    if not np.any(mask):
        mask[0] = True
    if mask.size == n:
        mask[0] = False
        if not np.any(mask):
            mask[np.argmax(a)] = True
    return mask


def random_mask_pass(a, p, rng, num_moves=80, time_limit=10.0,
                     sample_count=16, golden_iters=10):
    """Run a batch of exact group-scaling moves."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    start_time = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start_time + max(0.0, float(time_limit))
    improved = False
    for _ in range(max(1, int(num_moves))):
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
            break
        mask = choose_mask(a, p, rng, n)
        a, p, imp = mask_improve_one(
            a, p, mask,
            sample_count=sample_count,
            golden_iters=golden_iters
        )
        if imp:
            improved = True
    return a, p, improved


def block_sweep(a, p, B=16, time_limit=8.0, sample_count=14, golden_iters=10):
    """Sequentially optimize amplitudes of contiguous blocks."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    start_time = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start_time + max(0.0, float(time_limit))
    B = int(max(2, min(n // 2, max(4, B))))
    improved = False
    for start in range(0, n, n // B):
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
            break
        end = min(n, start + max(1, n // B))
        if end <= start:
            continue
        mask = np.zeros(n, dtype=bool)
        mask[start:end] = True
        a, p, imp = mask_improve_one(a, p, mask, sample_count, golden_iters)
        if imp:
            improved = True
    return a, p, improved


def try_gradient_direction(a, p, d, cur_peak, betas):
    """Try additive and multiplicative projected gradient moves."""
    d = np.asarray(d, dtype=np.float64)
    dm = d - float(np.mean(d))
    scale = float(np.max(np.abs(dm)))
    if not np.isfinite(scale) or scale <= 1e-300:
        return None, None
    dn = dm / scale
    best_a = None
    best_p = None
    best_pk = cur_peak
    for beta in betas:
        beta = float(beta)
        h = a - beta * dn
        h = normalize_nonneg(h)
        hp = convolution(h, h)
        ph = float(hp.max())
        if ph < best_pk * (1.0 - 1e-12):
            best_pk = ph
            best_a = h
            best_p = hp
        h2 = a * np.exp(-beta * dn)
        h2 = normalize_nonneg(h2)
        hp2 = convolution(h2, h2)
        ph2 = float(hp2.max())
        if ph2 < best_pk * (1.0 - 1e-12):
            best_pk = ph2
            best_a = h2
            best_p = hp2
    if best_a is None:
        return a, p
    return best_a, best_p


def gradient_improve(a, p, betas=(0.01, 0.02, 0.05, 0.1, 0.18, 0.3, 0.5)):
    """Smooth-max gradient descent steps (top-k and softmax)."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    cur = float(p.max())
    if cur <= 0.0:
        return a, p, False

    K = max(8, min(n * 4, p.size // 4, 128))
    w = topk_weights(p, K)
    d = gradient_from_weights(a, w)
    a, p, _ = None, None, None
    a, p = try_gradient_direction(a := a, p := p, d, cur, betas)
    if p is not None:
        improved = float(p.max()) < cur * (1.0 - 1e-12)
        if improved:
            cur = float(p.max())

    temps = [0.005, 0.02, 0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.4]
    for tf in temps:
        if cur <= 0.0:
            break
        w = softmax_weights(p, tf * cur + 1e-18)
        d = gradient_from_weights(a, w)
        a2, p2 = try_gradient_direction(a, p, d, cur, betas)
        if p2 is not None and float(p2.max()) < cur * (1.0 - 1e-12):
            a, p = a2, p2
            cur = float(p.max())
    return a, p, cur < float(convolution(a, a).max()) * (1.0 + 1e-12) and cur < float("inf")


def build_conv_matrix(a, rows):
    """Matrix A[row, j] = a[row - j] if valid, else 0."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    rows = np.asarray(rows, dtype=np.int64)
    A = np.zeros((rows.size, n), dtype=np.float64)
    for r, t in enumerate(rows):
        t = int(t)
        lo = max(0, t - n + 1)
        hi = min(n, t + 1)
        if hi > lo:
            idx = np.arange(lo, hi, dtype=np.int64)
            A[r, lo:hi] = a[t - idx]
    return A


def fstar_lp_improve(a, p, row_count=256, Mmult=1.0):
    """Classic LP direction: max sum g s.t. a*g <= M, g >= 0."""
    if linprog is None:
        return a, p, False
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n > 1536:
        return a, p, False

    M = float(p.max() * Mmult)
    if M <= 0.0:
        return a, p, False
    rows = top_rows(p, int(row_count))
    A = build_conv_matrix(a, rows)
    if A.size == 0 or not np.any(A):
        return a, p, False

    c = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    b = np.full(rows.size, M, dtype=np.float64)
    bounds = [(0.0, 100.0)] * n
    try:
        res = linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=bounds, method="highs")
    except Exception:
        return a, p, False

    if res is None or res.status != 0 or res.x is None:
        return a, p, False

    g = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
    g = np.clip(g, 0.0, None)
    s = float(g.sum())
    if s <= 1.0 + 1e-6:
        return a, p, False
    g = g / s

    cur_peak = float(p.max())
    best_a = a
    best_p = p
    best_peak = cur_peak
    for alpha in (0.02, 0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.3, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0):
        h = (1.0 - alpha) * a + alpha * g
        h = normalize_nonneg(h)
        hp = convolution(h, h)
        ph = float(hp.max())
        if ph < best_peak * (1.0 - 1e-12):
            best_peak = ph
            best_a = h
            best_p = hp
    improved = best_peak < cur_peak * (1.0 - 1e-12)
    if not improved:
        return a, p, False
    return best_a, best_p, True


def linearized_lp_improve(a, p, row_count=512):
    """
    Solve a linearization-of-square LP:
       minimize t
       s.t. 2(A_t a) - p_t <= t, sum a=1, a>=0.
    """
    if linprog is None:
        return a, p, False
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    m = 2 * n - 1
    if n > 1024:
        return a, p, False

    M = float(p.max())
    if M <= 0.0:
        return a, p, False

    rc = int(max(32, min(row_count, m)))
    rows = top_rows(p, rc)
    A = build_conv_matrix(a, rows)
    B = 2.0 * A

    A_ub = np.empty((B.shape[0], n + 1), dtype=np.float64)
    A_ub[:, :n] = B
    A_ub[:, n] = -1.0
    b_ub = p[rows].astype(np.float64)

    c = np.zeros(n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    c[n] = 1.0
    A_eq = np.zeros((1, n + 1), dtype=np.float64)
    A_eq[0, :n] = 1.0
    b_eq = np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)
    bounds = [(0.0, 1.0)] * n + [(0.0, max(1.0, 10.0 * M))]
    try:
        res = linprog(c, A_ub=A_ub, b_ub=b_ub, A_eq=A_eq, b_eq=b_eq,
                      bounds=bounds, method="highs")
    except Exception:
        return a, p, False

    if res is None or res.status != 0 or res.x is None:
        return a, p, False

    cand = np.asarray(res.x[:n], dtype=np.float64)
    cand = normalize_nonneg(cand)

    cur_peak = float(p.max())
    best_a = a
    best_p = p
    best_peak = cur_peak
    for alpha in (0.05, 0.1, 0.18, 0.3, 0.5, 0.7, 1.0):
        h = (1.0 - alpha) * a + alpha * cand
        h = normalize_nonneg(h)
        hp = convolution(h, h)
        ph = float(hp.max())
        if ph < best_peak * (1.0 - 1e-12):
            best_peak = ph
            best_a = h
            best_p = hp
    improved = best_peak < cur_peak * (1.0 - 1e-12)
    if not improved:
        return a, p, False
    return best_a, best_p, True


def perturb_sequence(a, rng, scale):
    """Multiplicative positive perturbation."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    h = a * np.exp(float(scale) * rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, size=n))
    h = normalize_nonneg(h)
    p = convolution(h, h)
    return h, p


def local_search(a, p, budget_s, rng, state, use_lp=True):
    """Main local improvement loop at fixed length."""
    a = normalize_nonneg(a)
    p = convolution(a, a)
    n = a.size
    update_best(state, a, p)

    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + max(0.0, float(budget_s))
    no_improve = 0
    moves_since_recompute = 0
    last_lp = 0.0
    loop = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        loop += 1
        now = time.monotonic()
        if now >= deadline:
            break

        if moves_since_recompute >= 25:
            a = normalize_nonneg(a)
            p = convolution(a, a)
            moves_since_recompute = 0

        cur_score = 2.0 * n * float(p.max())

        # Coordinate descent is exact and powerful for moderate lengths.
        if n <= 1024 and now < deadline - 5.0:
            rem = float(max(0.0, deadline - now))
            tl = min(25.0, max(3.0, rem * 0.12))
            if n <= 600:
                tl = min(35.0, tl * 1.3)
            sc = 16 if n <= 600 else 11
            gi = 12 if n <= 600 else 9
            a, p, imp = coordinate_pass(
                a, p, rng, max_passes=1, time_limit=tl,
                sample_count=sc, golden_iters=gi
            )
            moves_since_recompute += 5
            if imp:
                no_improve = 0
                update_best(state, a, p)
            else:
                no_improve += 1

        # Block sweeps are deterministic and repair block-amplitude imbalances.
        if n <= 2048 and now < deadline - 3.0:
            B = 8 if n > 1024 else 14
            a, p, imp = block_sweep(
                a, p, B=B,
                time_limit=min(8.0, max(2.0, float(deadline - now) * 0.06)),
                sample_count=12, golden_iters=8
            )
            moves_since_recompute += B
            if imp:
                no_improve = 0
                update_best(state, a, p)
            else:
                no_improve += 1

        # Random exact group scalings.
        if now < deadline - 2.0:
            rem = float(max(0.0, deadline - now))
            tl = min(30.0, max(3.0, rem * 0.25))
            num_moves = max(8, min(400, 220000 // max(1, n)))
            a, p, imp = random_mask_pass(
                a, p, rng, num_moves=num_moves, time_limit=tl,
                sample_count=14, golden_iters=9
            )
            moves_since_recompute += num_moves
            if imp:
                no_improve = 0
                update_best(state, a, p)
            else:
                no_improve += 1

        # Smooth-max gradient steps.
        if loop % 3 == 0 and now < deadline - 5.0 and n <= 2048:
            a, p, imp = gradient_improve(a, p)
            moves_since_recompute += 3
            if imp:
                no_improve = 0
                update_best(state, a, p)
            else:
                no_improve += 1

        # LP moves are expensive, so call them sparingly.
        if use_lp and n <= 1024 and now - last_lp > 60.0 and now < deadline - 15.0:
            last_lp = now
            a2, p2, imp2 = linearized_lp_improve(a, p, row_count=min(600, 2 * n - 1))
            if imp2:
                a, p = a2, p2
                no_improve = 0
                update_best(state, a, p)
            else:
                a2, p2, imp2 = fstar_lp_improve(a, p, row_count=min(256, 2 * n - 1), Mmult=1.0)
                if imp2:
                    a, p = a2, p2
                    no_improve = 0
                    update_best(state, a, p)
                else:
                    a2, p2, imp2 = fstar_lp_improve(a, p, row_count=min(128, 2 * n - 1), Mmult=1.03)
                    if imp2:
                        a, p = a2, p2
                        no_improve = 0
                        update_best(state, a, p)
                    else:
                        no_improve += 1

        # Escape from apparent local optima.
        if no_improve >= 4 and now < deadline - 5.0 and state.get("a") is not None:
            scale = 0.05 + 0.035 * min(5, no_improve // 2)
            ah, ph = perturb_sequence(a, rng, scale)
            hs = 2.0 * n * float(ph.max())
            bs = float(state.get("score", float("inf")))
            if np.isfinite(bs) and hs > bs * 1.6 + 0.1:
                a = normalize_nonneg(state["a"])
                p = convolution(a, a)
            else:
                a, p = ah, ph
            no_improve = 0
            update_best(state, a, p)

        if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
            break

    a = normalize_nonneg(a)
    p = convolution(a, a)
    update_best(state, a, p)
    return state.get("a"), state.get("score")


def load_provided_sequence():
    """Load global height_sequence_1 if available."""
    try:
        raw = height_sequence_1
    except Exception:
        return None
    try:
        a = np.asarray(raw, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    except Exception:
        return None
    if a is None or a.size == 0:
        return None
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return None
    return a / s


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    """
    Main entry point.  Searches for a coefficient sequence with low official
    score and returns the best list found within the time budget.
    """
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    hard_deadline = t0 + max(60.0, float(budget_s) - 70.0)

    state = {
        "score": float("inf"),
        "a": None,
        "n": 0,
        "last_print": float("inf")
    }

    provided = load_provided_sequence()
    if provided is not None and provided.size >= 2:
        add_candidate(state_tmp := [], provided)
        update_best(state, provided)

    # Guaranteed safe fallbacks.
    fallback2 = np.array([2.0 / 3.0, 1.0 / 3.0], dtype=np.float64)
    update_best(state, fallback2)
    update_best(state, np.ones(64, dtype=np.float64))

    # Choose initial working length.
    N0 = 512
    if provided is not None:
        if 256 <= provided.size <= 2048:
            N0 = int(provided.size)
    N0 = int(max(256, min(N0, 2048)))

    # Generate and sort initial candidates at the working length.
    candidates = []
    add_candidate(candidates, np.ones(N0, dtype=np.float64))
    if provided is not None:
        add_candidate(candidates, resize_sequence(provided, N0))

    for K in (2, 3, 4, 5, 8, 12, 16, 24):
        for _ in range(2):
            add_candidate(candidates, make_block_pattern(N0, K, rng))
    for _ in range(8):
        add_candidate(candidates, make_wave_pattern(N0, rng))
    for _ in range(8):
        add_candidate(candidates, make_smooth_noise(N0, rng))

    scores = np.array([c[0] for c in candidates], dtype=np.float64)
    order = np.argsort(scores)
    candidates = [candidates[int(i)] for i in order]

    # Quick multi-start refinement of the best few initial basins.
    quick_count = min(3, len(candidates))
    for i in range(quick_count):
        if time.monotonic() > hard_deadline - 200.0:
            break
        sc, a, p = candidates[i]
        if sc > max(2.2, state["score"] + 0.25):
            break
        budget = min(55.0, max(15.0, (hard_deadline - time.monotonic()) * 0.08))
        local_search(a, p, budget, rng, state, use_lp=False)

    # Intensive refinement of current best at its length.
    if state.get("a") is not None:
        rem = float(max(0.0, hard_deadline - time.monotonic()))
        budget1 = min(860.0, max(60.0, rem * 0.52))
        local_search(state["a"], convolution(state["a"], state["a"]),
                     budget1, rng, state, use_lp=True)

    # Try longer lengths by continuation.
    desired_lengths = []
    current_n = int(state.get("n", N0))
    if current_n <= 512:
        desired_lengths = [1024, 2048]
    elif current_n <= 1024:
        desired_lengths = [2048, 4096]
    elif current_n <= 2048:
        desired_lengths = [4096]

    for N in desired_lengths:
        if time.monotonic() > hard_deadline - 180.0:
            break
        rem = float(max(0.0, hard_deadline - time.monotonic()))
        if rem < 120.0:
            break
        budget = min(380.0, max(70.0, rem * 0.42))
        if N > 2048:
            budget = min(budget, 260.0)
        if state.get("a") is None:
            break
        a_start = resize_sequence(state["a"], N)
        local_search(a_start, convolution(a_start, a_start),
                     budget, rng, state, use_lp=(N <= 1024))

    # Final polish at best length.
    if state.get("a") is not None:
        rem = float(max(0.0, hard_deadline - time.monotonic()))
        if rem > 30.0:
            budget = min(220.0, rem - 20.0)
            local_search(state["a"], convolution(state["a"], state["a"]),
                         budget, rng, state, use_lp=True)

    a = state.get("a")
    if a is None or a.size < 2:
        a = fallback2

    a = normalize_nonneg(a)
    # Final official check (for logging).
    cand_list = [float(x) for x in a]
    off = official_score(cand_list)
    print(f"final internal score={state.get('score', float('inf')):.10f} "
          f"official={off:.10f} n={len(cand_list)}", flush=True)

    return cand_list
```
